1c:T5382e,<p>The ghosts came from <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/23-ghosts-vertex-operators-and-d26/faddeev-popov-ghosts/" class="wiki-link">Faddeev-Popov ghosts</a> as bookkeeping: a Jacobian for slicing out the gauge redundancy of the worldsheet metric. Here we quantize them and read off one number, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mtext>gh</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{\text{gh}}=-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7167em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord text mtight"><span class="mord mtight">gh</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>. That number is the tax the gauge symmetry charges, expressed as negative central charge. It has to be paid by exactly <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> free scalars, and that is where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> comes from (<a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/23-ghosts-vertex-operators-and-d26/total-central-charge-and-d-26/" class="wiki-link">Total central charge and D = 26</a>). Everything in this note is in service of extracting <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> honestly, sign by sign.</p>
<h2 id="from-covariant-to-conformal-gauge">From covariant to conformal gauge</h2>
<p>The ghost action from the Faddeev–Popov procedure is

<p>Two simplifications happen at once, and they are the whole reason the ghost CFT is easy:</p>
<ol>
<li>

mathnormal">b</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2474em;"><span accent mtight"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1944em;"><span class="mord accent mtight"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1944em;"><span class="mord mtight">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span </li>
</ol>
<p>Crucially, the action does not contain <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>ω</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\omega</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">ω</span></span></span></span> at all: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>b</mi><mrow><mi>z</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b_{zz}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c^z</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6644em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are <strong>Weyl-neutral</strong> with these index placements. That neutrality is exactly what will fix their conformal weights. From here we keep only the holomorphic sector; the barred copy is identical.</p>
<h2 id="equations-of-motion-and-opes">Equations of motion and OPEs</h2>
<p>Varying <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> gives <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover></msub><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>ˉ</mo></mover></msub><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial_{\bar z}b=\partial_{\bar z}c=0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2474em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0556em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord accent mtight"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="accent-body" style="left:-0.1944em;"><span class="mord mtight">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2474em;"><span class="mord accent mtight"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.5678em;"><span style="top:-2.7em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span style="left:-0.1944em;"><span class="mord mtight">ˉ</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span> alone. This is the defining feature of a chiral CFT — the dynamics is entirely in how singular the fields get as they collide.</p>
<p>Those collisions are the OPE. The propagator comes from the path-integral identity that inserting <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mi mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0379em;">δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> integrates to zero, which gives <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><msup><mi>δ</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>σ</mi><mo>−</mo><msup><mi>σ</mi><mo mathvariant="normal" lspace="0em" rspace="0em">′</mo></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial_{\bar z}c(\sigma)\,b(\sigma')=2\pi\delta^2(\sigma-\sigma')</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4033em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:2em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∼</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2484em;vertical-align:-0.4033em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mbin mtight">−</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.23em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="frac-line" style="border-bottom-width:0.04em;"></span></span><span style="top:-3.394em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.4033em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span>
These say <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> are conjugate — bringing one near the other produces a simple pole, exactly like <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> in ordinary quantum mechanics produce a commutator. There is <strong>no</strong> singularity in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b\,b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> or <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c\,c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span>: like fields do not contract.</p>
<p>One warning that runs through every computation below: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> <strong>anticommute</strong> (they are Grassmann). Sliding one past the other flips a sign, so the <em>order</em> in which fields are written is load-bearing. Two things generate signs: differentiating the pole <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/(z-w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">1/</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>, and hopping Grassmann fields past each other to bring a contracted pair adjacent. We will see the first does all the work in the central charge and the second, for this system, quietly lands on <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span></span></span></span> — but keeping both explicit is the only way to trust the result.</p>
<h2 id="the-stress-tensor">The stress tensor</h2>
<p>The ghost stress tensor, after normal ordering, is
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>c</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>b</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo><mo>+</mo><mtext> </mtext><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi>c</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>b</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T(z)=2:\!\partial c\,b\!:+\,:\!c\,\partial b\!: .</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.1056em;"></span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span>
The coefficient <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> is not adjustable: it is the unique choice that makes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> transform as the Weyl-neutral tensors <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>b</mi><mrow><mi>z</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> (weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span>) and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>) demanded by their indices — the general "<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> first-order system." We now verify those weights fall out of this <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span>.</p>
<h3 id="c-has-weight-h-1"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> has weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h=-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span></h3>
<p>Take <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T(z)c(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> and keep single contractions. Contracting the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> against <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> (using <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>∼</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b(z)c(w)\sim 1/(z-w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">1/</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>):
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>∼</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>∼</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi><msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>c</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T(z)\,c(w)\sim 2\frac{\partial c(z)}{z-w}-\frac{c(z)}{(z-w)^2}\sim -\frac{c(w)}{(z-w)^2}+\frac{\partial c(w)}{z-w}.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">4</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, which needs <strong>both</strong> fields of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T(z)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> contracted with both fields of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> — a double contraction.</p>
<p><strong>The single-pair contractions</strong>, obtained by differentiating <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo <p>(Differentiating with respect to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span></span></span></span> flips the sign; the two-derivative one strengthens to a cubic pole.)</p>
<p>For two normal-ordered bilinears <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi>X</mi><mi>Y</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">:\!XY\!:(z)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mi>U</mi><mi>V</mi><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">:\!UV\!:(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>, the fully double-contracted piece is <em>not</em> one product of contractions — it is the antisymmetric combination
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mtext> </mtext><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>X</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>U</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>Y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>V</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo>+</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>X</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>V</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>Y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>U</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-\,\langle X(z)U(w)\rangle\langle Y(z)V(w)\rangle+\langle X(z)V(w)\rangle\langle Y(z)U(w)\rangle .</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)⟩</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)⟩</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)⟩</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)⟩</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span>
The relative minus comes from anticommuting the Grassmann fields to bring each contracted pair adjacent: the crossed pairing (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>–<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">U</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span></span></span></span>–<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span></span></span></span>) needs one extra hop relative to the nested pairing (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">X</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span></span></span></span>–<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span></span></span></span>–<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">U</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span></span></span></span>).</p>
<p>Here is the twist that governs the whole computation. In each of the four terms, both fields of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span></span></span></span> or <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span></span></span></span> are the <em>same</em> species (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> are both <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span>-type; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> are both <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span>-type), and like fields do not contract. So the crossed pairing <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>X</mi><mi>U</mi><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>Y</mi><mi>V</mi><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\langle XU\rangle\langle YV\rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span> always pairs <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> or <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and vanishes; only the nested pairing <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>X</mi><mi>V</mi><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mi>Y</mi><mi>U</mi><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+\langle XV\rangle\langle YU\rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0785em;">X</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">V</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.2222em;">Y</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">U</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span> ever survives. The antisymmetric hop sign never actually fires as a difference between two live pairings. Every minus that builds up to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-13</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">13</span></span></span></span> therefore comes from the <em>other</em> source of signs: differentiating a propagator, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>z</mi></msub><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo style="height:1.2484em;vertical-align:-0.4033em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span <p>Apply the surviving pairing to each term:</p>

4\,A(z)A(w)&#x26;:\ 4\big[+\langle\partial c\,b\rangle\langle b\,\partial c\rangle\big]
=4\Big(-\tfrac{1}{(z-w)^2}\Big)\Big(+\tfrac{1}{(z-w)^2}\Big)=-\frac{4}{(z-w)^4},\\
2\,A(z)B(w)&#x26;:\ 2\big[+\langle\partial c\,\partial b\rangle\langle b\,c\rangle\big]
=2\Big(-\tfrac{2}{(z-w)^3}\Big)\Big(\tfrac{1}{z-w}\Big)=-\frac{4}{(z-w)^4},\\
2\,B(z)A(w)&#x26;:\ 2\big[+\langle c\,b\rangle\langle\partial b\,\partial c\rangle\big]
=2\Big(\tfrac{1}{z-w}\Big)\Big(-\tfrac{2}{(z-w)^3}\Big)=-\frac{4}{(z-w)^4},\\
B(z)B(w)&#x26;:\ \big[+\langle c\,\partial b\rangle\langle\partial b\,c\rangle\big]
=\Big(+\tfrac{1}{(z-w)^2}\Big)\Big(-\tfrac{1}{(z-w)^2}\Big)=-\frac{1}{(z-w)^4}.
\end{aligned}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:9.9298em;vertical-align:-4.7149em;"></span><span coefficient <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><menclose notation="box"><mstyle scriptlevel="0" displaystyle="false"><mstyle class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.6105em;vertical-align:-0.6261em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" <p><strong>Cross-check.</strong> A general first-order <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">bc</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> system where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> has weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lambda</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">λ</span></span></span></span> (so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> has weight <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>λ</mi></mrow><annotation class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mo style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. At <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span>: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">✓</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=1-3\cdot 9=-26\ \checkmark</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">3</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">⋅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7756em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord amsrm">✓</span></span></span></span>, matching the direct computation above.</p>
<p>A negative central charge is not a problem here. The central charge is the <strong>Weyl anomaly coefficient</strong> of the CFT — the quantum failure of the trace <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>T</mi><mi>α</mi></msup><msub><mrow></mrow><mi>α</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T^\alpha{}_\alpha</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord"></span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0037em;">α</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> to vanish on a curved worldsheet. A unitary CFT has <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>></mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c>0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5782em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>; the ghosts are non-unitary by construction (anticommuting integer-spin fields are precisely the spin-statistics-violating objects), so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>&#x3C;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c&#x3C;0</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5782em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">&#x3C;</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span> is allowed and there is no contradiction. This negative <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> is the whole point: it is what cancels the positive <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=D</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span></span></span></span> of the matter sector.</p>
<h2 id="where--26-really-comes-from">Where <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> really comes from</h2>
<p>The <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> is not an arithmetic coincidence. It is forced by the weights <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo Traced back to <a href="/rome-wiki/articles/string-theory/part-v-graduate-bridge/23-ghosts-vertex-operators-and-d26/faddeev-popov-ghosts/" class="wiki-link">Faddeev-Popov ghosts</a>, the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> is a <strong>fingerprint of two-dimensional diffeomorphism invariance itself</strong>. The ghosts are the gauge symmetry made into fields, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> is the price tag it stamps on the theory.</p>
<h2 id="open-questions">Open questions</h2>
<ul>
<li>This derives only the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lambda=2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">λ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> diffeomorphism ghost. The fermionic formula <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mo superstring adds a <em>bosonic</em> superconformal <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mord">11</span></span></span></span>. None of this is needed for the bosonic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">D=26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">D</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> route.</li>
<li>Normal ordering <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>:</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>⋯</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mo>:</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">:\!\cdots\!:</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="minner">⋯</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span></span></span></span> here is holomorphic/radial ordering. Its precise relation to oscillator normal ordering is standard CFT technology, best revisited in a BRST-focused note.</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="exercise">Exercise</h2>
<p>A tempting but wrong story says the minus in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mtext>gh</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{\text{gh}}=-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7167em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord text mtight"><span class="mord mtight">gh</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span> comes from the Grassmann hop sign. Test it. First redo <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B(z)B(w)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em;">z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0269em;">w</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> pretending <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">b,c</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> are ordinary commuting fields but keeping the <em>same</em> propagators — so the double contraction is the symmetric sum with no hop sign. Does the answer change? Then say where the true sign difference between the fermionic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">bc</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span></span></span></span> system (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>) and a genuinely bosonic <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(2,-1)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> system (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=+26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>) actually lives, and tie it back to the Faddeev–Popov determinant.</p>
<details class="callout callout-success">
<summary class="callout-title">Solution</summary>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo Like fields don't contract, so the only surviving pairing is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.52em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span>
Dropping the Grassmann statistics changes <em>nothing</em>: the crossed pairing that the hop sign would have weighted still pairs <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">cc</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">cc</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>b</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">∂</mi><mi>b</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\partial b\,\partial b</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">∂</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span></span></span></span> and vanishes, and the surviving nested pairing carries a <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span></span></span></span> under both statistics. The whole minus is already there in the derivative on the propagator. The same is true term by term, so this commuting toy — same propagators, no hop — still totals <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-13</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">13</span></span></span></span> and gives <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c=-26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>, not <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">+26</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">+</span><span class="mord">26</span></span></span></span>. The Grassmann hop is a genuine part of the machinery but is not the origin of the sign.
<strong>Where the real flip lives.</strong> A true bosonic ghost system is the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>β</mi><mi>γ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\beta\gamma</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0528em;">β</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0556em;">γ</span></span></span></span> system, and its propagator is <em>antisymmetric</em>: <span class="mopen nulldelimiter"></span><span class="mfrac"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8451em;"><span style="top:-2.655em;"><span
